and hence
\mathbb{A}
come with actions of the spherical Weyl group
W_{s}
, the translations
T\coloneqq\mathbb{A}
and thus by the affine Weyl group
W_{a}\coloneqq T\rtimes W_{s}
. The spherical Weyl group can be identified with
N_{\mathbb{F}}/M_{\mathbb{F}}
,
[
App25
, Proposition
]
, which acts by conjugation on
A_{\mathbb{F}}
and on
A_{\Lambda}
. We now verify that the identification
f\colon\mathbb{A}\to A_{\Lambda}
is compatible with these actions. For roots
\alpha,\beta\in\Sigma
, the reflection
s_{\alpha}
along the hyperplane defined by
\alpha
is characterized by

\beta(s_{\alpha}(H))=\beta\left(H-\alpha(H)\alpha^{\vee}\right)=\beta(H)-\beta(\alpha^{\vee})\cdot\alpha(H)

for all
H\in\mathfrak{a}
. Using
[
App25
, Lemma
]
this can be translated to the multiplicative setting, where we can say
n\in N_{\mathbb{F}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Nor}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})

acts like the reflection

s_{\alpha}
if for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
,

\chi_{\beta}(nan^{-1})=\frac{\chi_{\beta}(a)}{\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{\beta(\alpha^{\vee})}}.

Lemma 4.15
.

For every reflection
s_{\alpha}
where
\alpha\in\Sigma
there is some
n\in N_{\mathbb{F}}
which acts like the reflection
s_{\alpha}
. Moreover, for all
x\in\mathbb{A}
, if
[a]=f(x)
for some
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, then
f(s_{\alpha}(x))=[nan^{-1}]\in A_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

There is some
n\in N_{\mathbb{F}}
that acts like the reflection
s_{\alpha}
by
[
App25
, Proposition
]
.
Let
x\in\mathbb{A}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
f(x)=[a]\in A_{\Lambda}
. By Lemma
4.13
, for all
\gamma\in L\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Sigma)
,
\gamma(x)=(-v)(\chi_{\gamma}(a))
. We verify that for all
\gamma\in L

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}([nan^{-1}])(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}_{\gamma}([nan^{-1}])=(-v)(\chi_{\gamma}(nan^{-1}))=(-v)\left(\frac{\chi_{\gamma}(a)}{\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{\gamma(\alpha^{\vee})}}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}_{\gamma}(f(x))-\gamma(\alpha^{\vee})\overline{\chi}_{\alpha}(f(x))=\gamma(x)-\gamma(\alpha^{\vee})\alpha(x)

\displaystyle=\gamma(x-\alpha(x)\alpha^{\vee})=\gamma(s_{\alpha}(x))=\overline{\varphi}(s_{\alpha}(x))(\gamma),

confirming
f(s_{\alpha}(x))=[nan^{-1}]
.
∎

4.5.
Valuation properties of
A_{\mathbb{F}}

In this subsection we investigate the connection of the matrix entries of elements in
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
with their characters
\chi_{\alpha}(a)
. For any matrix
a\in\mathbb{F}^{n\times n}
with matrix entries
(a_{ij})
let

(-v)(a)\coloneqq\max_{i,j}\{(-v)(a_{ij})\}.

Lemma 4.16
.

For all
a,a_{i}\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
for
i=1,\ldots,k
and
k\in\operatorname{SO}_{n}(\mathbb{F})
we have

(i)

(-v)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-v)(a_{i})
,

(ii)

(-v)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\right)\leq\max_{i}\{(-v)(a_{i})\}
,

(iii)

(-v)(k)=0
,

(iv)

(-v)(kak^{-1})=(-v)(a)
,

(v)

(-v)(a^{q})=q\cdot(-v)(a)
for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

For any matrices
a,b\in\mathbb{F}^{n\times n}
, we note that the valuation of the matrix entry
(ab)_{ik}
is bounded by
(-v)(a)
and
(-v)(b)

\displaystyle(-v)((ab)_{ik})

\displaystyle=(-v)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}b_{jk}\right)\leq\max_{j=1}^{n}\left\{(-v)(a_{ij})+(-v)(b_{jk})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{ij}\left\{(-v)(a_{ij})\right\}+\max_{ij}\left\{(-v)(b_{ij})\right\}.

This estimate extends to finite products of matrices by induction, proving (i).

For (ii), we observe

\displaystyle(-v)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{j,\ell}\left\{(-v)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}(a_{i})_{j\ell}\right)\right\}\leq\max_{j,\ell,i}\left\{(-v)\left((a_{i})_{j\ell}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\{(-v)(a_{i})\}.

For
k\in\operatorname{SO}_{n}(\mathbb{F})
, we have
(-v)(k)\leq 0
, since all entries satisfy
|k_{ij}|\leq 1
. Statement (iii) holds, since there is at least one matrix entry of
k
with valuation
0
, since otherwise the determinant of
k
would have to have negative valuation, since the set of elements in
\mathbb{F}
with negative valuation is an ideal.

By (i) and (iii) we have that
(-v)(kak^{-1})\leq(-v)(k)+(-v)(a)+(-v)(k^{-1})=(-v)(a)
, but replacing
a
by
k^{-1}ak
we also obtain the other inequality of (iv).

Recall that
a{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}=a
for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, so by the spectral theorem there exists
k\in\operatorname{SO}_{n}
such that
kak^{-1}
is a diagonal matrix. Note that when
q\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
, then
a^{q}
is defined as
k^{-1}(kak^{-1})^{q}k
. We then have

(-v)(a^{q})=(-v)\left(ka^{q}k^{-1}\right)=(-v)\left(\left(kak^{-1}\right)^{q}\right)=q\cdot(-v)\left(kak^{-1}\right)=q\cdot(-v)(a)

for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}
, concluding the proof of (v).
∎

The following description of
A_{\mathbb{F}}
in terms of matrix entries comes from the semialgebraic definition of
A_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Lemma 4.17
.

There exists a constant
C\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, such that for all
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{\geq 1}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, if for all
\delta\in\Delta
,
\chi_{\delta}(a)\leq\varepsilon
, then
(-v)(a)\leq C\cdot(-v)(\varepsilon)
.

There is also a constant
C^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, such that for all
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\geq 0}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, if for all
\delta\in\Delta
,
(-v)(\chi_{\delta}(a))\leq\lambda
, then
(-v)(a)\leq C^{\prime}\cdot\lambda
.

Proof.

Since both
t_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{F}_{>0}\to A_{\mathbb{F}}
and the projection
\pi_{ij}\colon A_{\mathbb{F}}\to\mathbb{F}
to the
ij
-matrix entry are semi-algebraic defined over
\mathbb{K}
, the their concatenation
\mathbb{F}_{>0}\to\mathbb{F}
is semi-algebraic and it’s growth is bounded by a polynomial
p\in\mathbb{K}[x]
in the sense that for all
\alpha\in\Sigma
and all
x\in\mathbb{F}
we have

-p(x)\leq t_{\alpha}(x)_{ij}\leq p(x).

Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{\geq 1}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
\chi_{\delta}(a)\leq\varepsilon
for all
\delta\in\Delta
. By Proposition
4.9
there exist
q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Q}
such that

a=\prod_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}t_{\delta}(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a))^{q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}}.

We apply the above estimate and Lemma
4.16
to obtain

\displaystyle(-v)\left(a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}(-v)\left(t_{\delta}(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a))^{q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}}\right)=\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}(-v)\left(t_{\delta}(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}(-v)\left(p(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a))\right)

and if
p(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\deg(p)}b_{n}x^{n}\in\mathbb{K}[x]
, then

\displaystyle(-v)(p(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a)))

\displaystyle\leq\max_{n}\left\{(-v)\left(b_{n}\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a)^{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\deg(p)\cdot(-v)(\chi_{\delta^{\prime}}(a)),0\right\}\leq\deg(p)\cdot(-v)(\varepsilon),

so

\displaystyle(-v)(a)\leq\left(\deg(p)\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right)(-v)(\varepsilon)\leq\left\lvert\deg(p)\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right\rvert(-v)(\varepsilon),

so we can define

C\coloneqq\left\lvert\deg(p)\sum_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta}q_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right\rvert

to conclude the first part of the proof. For the second statement, assume we have
\lambda\in\Lambda_{>0}
,
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
with
(-v)(\chi_{\delta}(a))\leq\lambda
for all
\delta\in\Delta
. Choose
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{>1}
such that
(-v)(\varepsilon)=2\lambda
. Then
(-v)(\chi_{\delta}(a))\leq\lambda<(-v)(\varepsilon)
, so
\chi_{\delta}(a)<\varepsilon
and the first part can be applied to obtain
(-v)(a)\leq C\cdot(-v)(\varepsilon)=C\cdot 2\lambda\eqcolon C^{\prime}\cdot\lambda
for
C^{\prime}=2C
.
∎

4.6.
Stabilizer of a point

We denote the equivalence class of
\operatorname{Id}
by
o\in\mathcal{B}
. The stabilizer of
o
has been calculated by
[
Tho02
]
when
\mathbb{F}
is a Robinson field and by
[
KT02
]
more generally. We first describe the special case of the action of
A_{\mathbb{F}}
on
\mathcal{B}
. For any semi-algebraic subset
H_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq G_{\mathbb{F}}
we write
H_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\coloneqq H_{\mathbb{F}}\cap O^{n\times n}
, where
O
is the valuation ring.

Proposition 4.18
.

The following are equivalent for
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
.

(i)

a\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
.

(ii)

N_{\mathbb{F}}(a)\in O
.

(iii)

\chi_{\alpha}(a)\in O\ \forall\alpha\in\Sigma
.

(iv)

\chi_{\alpha}(a)\in O^{\times}\ \forall\alpha\in\Delta
.

(v)

a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Moreover, the apartment can be identified with
\mathbb{A}\cong A_{\Lambda}\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}/A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}.o
.

Proof.

If
a\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
, then
d(\operatorname{Id},a.\operatorname{Id})=(-v)(N_{\mathbb{F}}(a))=0
, so
N_{\mathbb{F}}(a)\in O
. This implies by the definition of
N_{\mathbb{F}}
that
\chi_{\alpha}(a)\in O\cap O^{-1}=O^{\times}
for all
\alpha\in\Sigma
, in particular for all
\alpha\in\Delta
. There is some
\varepsilon\in O\cap\mathbb{F}_{\geq 1}
such that
\chi_{\alpha}(a)\leq\varepsilon
for all
\alpha\in\Delta
, so by Lemma
4.17
, the valuations of the matrix entries of
a
are all bounded by
(-v)(\varepsilon)=0
, so
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.
On the other hand, if
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, then the linear map
\operatorname{Ad}(a)\colon\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g},X\mapsto aXa^{-1}
restricts to multiplication by
\chi_{\alpha}(a)
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
, from which can be concluded that
\chi_{\alpha}(a)\in O
. Then
N_{\mathbb{F}}(a)\in O
and
d(o,a.o)=0
, so
a\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
.

By Section
2.3
,
\mathbb{A}\cong A_{\Lambda}\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}/\{a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\colon N(a)\in O\}\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}/\operatorname{Stab}_{A_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)=A_{\mathbb{F}}/A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, explicitly
x\in\mathbb{A}
corresponds to
a.o=aa{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}=a^{2}
if and only if
f(x)=[a^{2}]
in the language of Theorem
4.12
and by the orbit stabilizer theorem for the action of
A_{\mathbb{F}}
on
\mathcal{B}
, we have
A_{\mathbb{F}}.o\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}/\operatorname{Stab}_{A_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)\cong\mathbb{A}
.
∎

Theorem 4.19
.

The stabilizer of
o\in\mathcal{B}
in
G_{\mathbb{F}}
is
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Proof.

Let
g=kak^{\prime}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}=K_{\mathbb{F}}A^{+}_{\mathbb{F}}K_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
g\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
, then
d(\operatorname{Id},g.\operatorname{Id})=(-v)(N_{\mathbb{F}}(a))=0
. By Proposition
4.18
,
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Now since
K_{\mathbb{F}}=K_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
,
g=kak^{\prime}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

If on the other hand we start with a
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, then
a\in\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, and by Proposition
4.18
,
N_{\mathbb{F}}(a)\in O
and thus also
d(\operatorname{Id},g.\operatorname{Id})=d(\operatorname{Id},a.\operatorname{Id})=0
, hence
g\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
.
∎

As an application of the Iwasawa retraction, Theorem
4.4
, we can give a group decomposition for the stabilizer of
o
in
\mathcal{B}
.

Corollary 4.20
.

There is an Iwasawa decomposition
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)K_{\mathbb{F}}
, meaning that for every
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
there are unique
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
,
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
,
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}=K_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
with
g=uak
.

Proof.

Let
g=uak\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\subseteq U_{\mathbb{F}}A_{\mathbb{F}}K_{\mathbb{F}}
. We have
\rho(g.\operatorname{Id})=a.\operatorname{Id}
. Since
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, we have by Theorem
4.4

\displaystyle d(\operatorname{Id},a.\operatorname{Id})

\displaystyle=d(\operatorname{Id},\rho(g.\operatorname{Id}))\leq d(\operatorname{Id},g.\operatorname{Id})=0.

This means that
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\cap G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Note that since
K_{\mathbb{F}}
stabilizes
\operatorname{Id}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}
,
K_{\mathbb{F}}=K_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Since
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
is a subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{F}}
,
u=gk^{-1}a^{-1}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, so
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.
∎

5.
Verification of the axioms for
\mathcal{B}

We continue in the setting of Section
4
:
\mathbb{K}
and
\mathbb{F}
are real closed fields such that
\mathbb{K}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\cap\mathbb{F}
and
\mathbb{F}
is non-Archimedean with an order compatible valuation
v
. Let
G<\operatorname{SL}_{n}
be a semisimple connected self-adjoint algebraic
\mathbb{K}
-group. Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}/\!\sim
as in Section
4.3
and
\mathbb{A}\cong A_{\mathbb{F}}.o\subseteq\mathcal{B}
as in Proposition
4.18
. Denote the inclusion by
f_{0}\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathcal{B}
. Explicitly, in terms of Theorem
4.12
, we have for all
x\in\mathbb{A}
,
f_{0}(x)=a.o
if and only if
f(x)=[a^{2}]
. We define a set of charts

\mathscr{F}=\left\{g.f_{0}\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathcal{B}\colon g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}\right\}.

The goal of this section is to show that
(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{F})
is an affine
\Lambda
-building in the sense of Section
2.4
. Recall that a root system
\Phi
is called
reduced
if
\alpha\in\Phi
implies
2\alpha\notin\Phi
. Recall that the root system
\Sigma
of the Lie group
G_{\mathbb{R}}
coincides with the root system
\vphantom{\Phi}{}_{\mathbb{K}}\Phi
of the algebraic group
G
relative to an
\mathbb{F}
-split torus.

Theorem 5.1
.

If the root system
\Sigma
of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is reduced, then the pair
(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{F})
is an affine
\Lambda
-building of type
\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{A}(\Sigma^{\vee},\Lambda,T)
, where
T=\mathbb{A}
is the full translation group.

We will show the theorem by checking the set of axioms (A1), (A2), (A3), (A4), (TI), (EC), as described in Theorem
2.3
. Axioms (A1), (A3) and (TI) are treated in Section
5.1
and follow easily from what we have developed so far.
Before proving the other axioms, we will develop some theory. We will introduce explicit root group valuations to investigate the action of the root groups
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. This results in partial results about
W_{a}
-convexity and allows us to describe the pointwise stabilizers of the fundamental Weyl chamber
C_{0}
, the entire apartment
\mathbb{A}
and half-apartments
H_{\alpha}
. We will then follow some ideas of
[
BT72
,
Lan96
]
, that allow us to describe the stabilizers of arbitrary finite subsets of
\mathbb{A}
. In Section
5.8
we first show the change of charts condition of axiom (A2), which we then use to describe the stabilizers of arbitrary (not necessarily finite) subsets of
\mathbb{A}
, which in turn allows us to conclude the proof of axiom (A2). Finally, axioms (A4) and (EC) are proven in Sections
5.9
and
5.10
.

We develop much of the theory also for the case where
\Sigma
may not be reduced, and explicitly mention when reduced
\Sigma
is needed.
The assumption that
\Sigma
is reduced is directly used in the proof of axioms (A2) and (EC). Axiom (A4) uses the assumption indirectly, as it relies on the statement of (A2) in its proof. The remaining axioms (A1), (A3) and (TI) do not need the assumption. Further discussion of this assumption can be found in Section
5.11
.
An alternative, much simpler proof of axiom (A2) in the case of
G_{\mathbb{F}}=\operatorname{SL}(n,\mathbb{F})
is given in Appendix
7
.

5.1.
Axioms (A1), (A3) and (TI)

Three of the axioms follow directly from what we have done.

Lemma 5.2
.

The pair
(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{F})
satisfies axiom

(A1)

For all
f\in\mathscr{F}
,
w\in W_{a}
,
f\circ w\in\mathscr{F}
.

Proof.

Let
w=(t^{x},w_{s})\in W_{a}=T\rtimes W_{s}
, where
t
is a translation by
x\in\mathbb{A}
and
w_{s}=s_{1}\cdots s_{k}
is a product of reflections. Let
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
f_{0}(x)=a.o
as in Theorem
4.12
. Let
n_{i}\in N_{\mathbb{F}}
that act like
s_{i}
as in Lemma
4.15
and let
n=n_{1}\cdots n_{k}
. Let
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
with
g.f_{0}=f
.

Now for all
y\in\mathbb{A}
, let
b_{i}\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
f_{0}(s_{i}\cdots s_{k}(y))=b_{i}.o
. Then by Corollary
4.14
and Lemma
4.15

\displaystyle f\circ w(y)

\displaystyle=g.f_{0}(t^{x}(w_{s}(y)))=g.f_{0}(x+w_{s}(y))=g.ab_{1}.o=ga.f_{0}(w_{s}(y))

\displaystyle=ga.f_{0}(s_{1}\cdots s_{k}(y))=ga.n_{1}.b_{2}.o=gan_{1}.f_{0}(s_{2}\cdots s_{k}(y))=\cdots

\displaystyle=gan_{1}\cdots n_{k}.f_{0}(y)=gan.f_{0}(y),

so for
\tilde{g}\coloneqq gan\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
we have
f\circ w=\tilde{g}.f_{0}\in\mathscr{F}
, proving axiom (A1).
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

The pair
(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{F})
satisfies axiom

(A3)

For all
p,q\in\mathcal{B}
, there exists
f\in\mathscr{F}
such that
p,q\in f(\mathbb{A})
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.1
(c): if
[x],[y]\in\mathcal{B}
, for points
x,y\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}
, then there is a
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
g.x=\operatorname{Id}\in A_{\mathbb{F}}.\operatorname{Id}
and
g.y\in A_{\mathbb{F}}.\operatorname{Id}
. Then
[x],[y]\in g^{-1}.f_{0}(\mathbb{A})
for the chart
g^{-1}.f_{0}\in\mathscr{F}
.
∎

Axiom (TI) just states that
\mathcal{B}
satisfies the triangle inequality. The triangle inequality was proven in Theorem
4.5
using Kostant convexity and Iwasawa-retractions.

5.2.
Valuation properties of
U_{\mathbb{F}}

Recall from
[
App25
, Section
]
, that
U_{\mathbb{F}}
has subgroups
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
for
\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}
defined as
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=\exp((\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\oplus(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})
. Since
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
is unipotent, the matrix exponential
\exp\colon(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\oplus(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\to(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and the matrix logarithm

\displaystyle\log\colon(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\to(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\oplus(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k+1}\frac{(u-\operatorname{Id})^{k}}{k}

are just polynomials on
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. Viewing
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{F})\subseteq\mathbb{F}^{n\times n}
, we can speak about the matrix entries
Z_{ij}
of
Z\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
. Let as in Section
4.5

(-v)(Z):=\max_{ij}\{(-v)(Z_{ij})\}.

Inspired by
[
BT72
, 6.2]
and in order to avoid talking about matrix entries too much we introduce the
root group valuations

\varphi_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}\colon(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\to\Lambda\cup\{-\infty\}

\displaystyle\exp(X+X^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mapsto\max\left\{\max_{i,j}\left\{(-v)(X_{ij})\right\},\frac{1}{2}\max_{i,j}\left\{(-v)(X^{\prime}_{ij})\right\}\right\}

where
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
X^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. The following Lemma justifies their name. Readers familiar with Bruhat-Tits theory may notice that we have chosen the opposite sign convention to simplify the notation.

Lemma 5.4
.

For all
\alpha\in\Sigma
and
u,v\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\varphi_{\alpha}(uv)\leq\max\left\{\varphi_{\alpha}(u),\varphi_{\alpha}(v)\right\}

and if
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\neq\varphi_{\alpha}(v)
, then equality holds.

Proof.

We first claim that for all
X,Y\in\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
,

\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(\left[X,Y\right]\right)\leq\max\{(-v)(X),(-v)(Y)\}.

Indeed in matrix entries,
[X,Y]_{ij}=\sum_{k}X_{ik}Y_{kj}-Y_{ik}X_{kj}
, and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(\left[X,Y\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\max_{ij}\left\{(-v)\left(\sum_{k}X_{ik}Y_{kj}-Y_{ik}X_{kj}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\max_{ijk}\left\{(-v)(X_{ik}Y_{kj}),(-v)(Y_{ik}X_{kj})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\max_{ijk}\{(-v)(X_{ik})+(-v)(Y_{kj}),(-v)(Y_{ik})+(-v)(X_{kj})\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\max\{(-v)(X)+(-v)(Y),(-v)(Y)+(-v)(X)\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\max\{2(-v)(X),2(-v)(Y)\}=\max\{(-v)(X),(-v)(Y)\}.

Now let
u=\exp(X+X^{\prime})
and
v=\exp(Y+Y^{\prime})
for
X,Y\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. Then

u\cdot v=\exp\left(X+Y+X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)

by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff-formula
[
App25
, Proposition
]
. By the claim

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}(uv)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)(X+Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{(-v)(X),(-v)(Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)(X^{\prime}),\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\{\varphi_{\alpha}(u),\varphi_{\alpha}(v)\}.

Without loss of generality
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)<\varphi_{\alpha}(v)
and we distinguish two cases. If
\varphi_{\alpha}(v)=(-v)(Y)\geq\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})
, then
(-v)(X)<(-v)(Y)
and
\frac{1}{2}(-v)(X^{\prime})<(-v)(Y)
. Thus using the claim

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}(uv)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)(Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)(Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)(X^{\prime}),\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime}),(-v)(X),(-v)(Y)\right\}

\displaystyle=(-v)(Y)=\varphi_{\alpha}(v).

If on the other hand
\varphi_{\alpha}(v)=\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})>(-v)(Y)
, then
(-v)(X)<\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})
and
(-v)(X^{\prime})<(-v)(Y^{\prime})
. Thus using the claim

\displaystyle(-v)\left(X^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{(-v)(X^{\prime}),2(-v)(X),2(-v)(Y)\right\}<(-v)(Y^{\prime})

and

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}(uv)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)(X+Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(Y^{\prime}+X^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)(X+Y),\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})\right\}=\frac{1}{2}(-v)(Y^{\prime})=\varphi_{\alpha}(v).\qed

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
B_{\theta}
be the scalar product defined on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
in
[
App25
, Section
]
, then

(-v)(X)=(-v)\left(\sqrt{B_{\theta}(X,X)}\right)

for all
X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
X,Y\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
are orthogonal with respect to
B_{\theta}
, then

(-v)(X+Y)=\max\{(-v)(X),(-v)(Y)\}.

Proof.

We note that
(-v)(X)=(-v)(\lVert X\rVert_{\infty})
with the supremum norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty}
. Since on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{R}}
, all norms are equivalent, we can use the transfer principle to obtain a constant
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}

\frac{1}{k}\sqrt{B_{\theta}(X,X)}\leq\lVert X\rVert_{\infty}\leq k\sqrt{B_{\theta}(X,X)},

from which
(-v)(X)=(-v)\left(\lVert X\rVert_{\infty}\right)=(-v)\left(\sqrt{B_{\theta}(X,X)}\right)
follows.

Now if
X,Y\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
are orthogonal,
B_{\theta}(X,Y)=0
, then

\displaystyle(-v)(X+Y)

\displaystyle=(-v)\left(\sqrt{B_{\theta}(X+Y,X+Y)}\right)=\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(B_{\theta}(X,X)+B_{\theta}(Y,Y)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\max\left\{(-v)(B_{\theta}(X,X)),(-v)(B_{\theta}(Y,Y))\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\{(-v)(X),(-v)(Y)\}

where we used positive definiteness to see that
B_{\theta}(X,X)
and
B_{\theta}(Y,Y)
do not cancel.
∎

The root group valuation allows us to describe when an element of
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
fixes the base point
o\in\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma
and
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. The following are equivalent

(i)

u.o=o
,

(ii)

\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq 0
,

(iii)

u=\exp(X+X^{\prime})
for some
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O),\ X^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
,

(iv)

\log(u)\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Moreover
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)<0
if and only if
(-v)\left((u-\operatorname{Id})_{ij}\right)<0
for all
i,j
.

Proof.

Let
u=\exp(X+X^{\prime})
with
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},X^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. By Theorem
4.19
, (i)
u.o=o
is equivalent to
u\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
which by applying the logarithm, which is a polynomial, is equivalent to
X+X^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, (iv). By
[
App25
, Proposition
]
the root space decomposition is orthogonal with respect to the Killing form. Since
X
and
X^{\prime}
lie orthogonal to each other,
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
individually (iii), by Lemma
5.5
. It is then clear that all the matrix entries of
X
and
X^{\prime}
lie in
O
, hence (ii)
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq 0
. All these implications are equivalences.

If
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)<0
, then
(-v)(X_{ij})<0
and
(-v)(X_{ij}^{\prime})<0
. Then

(-v)\left((u-\operatorname{Id})_{ij}\right)=(-v)\left(\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(X+X^{\prime})^{k}}{k!}\right)_{ij}\right)<0.

If
(-v)((u-\operatorname{Id})_{ij})<0
, then

(-v)((X+X^{\prime})_{ij})=(-v)(\log(u)_{ij})=(-v)\left(\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k+1}\frac{(u-\operatorname{Id})^{k}}{k}\right)_{ij}\right)<0,

so with Lemma
5.5
,
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)<0
.
∎

5.3.
W_{a}
-convexity for
U_{\mathbb{F}}

We first use the Iwasawa retraction to show that if
p\in\mathbb{A}
is sent to
q\in\mathbb{A}
by an element
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
, then
p=q
.

Proposition 5.7
.

For all
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}

ua.o\in\mathbb{A}\iff ua.o=a.o.

Proof.

For all
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
ua.o=a.o
, then clearly
ua.o\in\mathbb{A}
. We now have to show the converse. So let
ua.o\in\mathbb{A}
, meaning that there exists
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
d(ua.o,b.o)=0
. Since the Iwasawa retraction
\rho
is distance diminishing by Theorem
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle d(b.o,a.o)

\displaystyle=d(b.\operatorname{Id},a.\operatorname{Id})=d(\rho(b.\operatorname{Id}),\rho(u.a.\operatorname{Id}))

\displaystyle\leq d(b.\operatorname{Id},ua.\operatorname{Id})=d(b.o,ua.o)=0,

and thus
a.o=b.o=ua.o\in\mathbb{A}
, as claimed.
∎

We now show that the set of points in
\mathbb{A}
that are fixed by an element
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
is a half-apartment and in particular
W_{a}
-convex.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma
. For
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
we have

\left\{p\in\mathbb{A}\colon u.p\in\mathbb{A}\right\}=\left\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)\right)\right\}

and therefore this set is a half-apartment (with a wall parallel to the wall defined by
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha})=0
) when
u\neq\operatorname{Id}
.

Proof.

Let
p=a.o\in\mathbb{A}
and
u=\exp(X+X^{\prime})
with
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},X^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. We have

\displaystyle ua.o\in\mathbb{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Prop. }\ref{prop:UonA}}}{{\iff}}

\displaystyle ua.o=a.o\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Thm. }\ref{thm:stab}}}{{\iff}}a^{-1}ua\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle a^{-1}\exp\left(X+X^{\prime}\right)a\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{
\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{AppAGRCF}{}{}, Lemma \ref{I-lem:aexpXa}]}
}}}{{\iff}}

\displaystyle\exp\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)^{-1}X+\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{-2}X^{\prime}\right)\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O).

Denoting
u^{\prime}:=\exp\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)^{-1}X+\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{-2}X^{\prime}\right)
, by Lemma
5.6
, the above are equivalent to
{\varphi_{\alpha}(u^{\prime})\leq 0}
. Using the abbreviation
(-v)(Z):=\max_{ij}\{(-v)(Z_{ij})\}
for
Z\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{F}}
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{-1}X\right),\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}(a)^{-2}X^{\prime}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{(-v)\left(X\right),\frac{1}{2}(-v)\left(X^{\prime}\right)\right\}-(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)-(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\leq 0

Thus we see that
ua.o\in\mathbb{A}
is equivalent to
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))
.
∎

Before upgrading the previous result to all of
U_{\mathbb{F}}
, we consider what happens to products in
U_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\eta\in\Sigma_{>0},u\in\exp((\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\mathbb{F}})
and
u^{\prime}\in\exp(\bigoplus_{\alpha>\eta}(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})
. If
uu^{\prime}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, then
u\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
and
u^{\prime}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Proof.

For
X=\log(u)
and
Y=\log(u^{\prime})
, we consider the BCH-formula from
[
App25
, Proposition
]

\exp(X)\exp(Y)=\exp\left(X+Y+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]+\frac{1}{12}\left(\left[X,\left[X,Y\right]\right]+\left[Y,\left[Y,X\right]\right]\right)+\ldots\right)

Now if
uu^{\prime}=\exp(X)\exp(Y)\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, then

\log(uu^{\prime})=X+Y+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]+\frac{1}{12}([X,[X,Y]]+[Y,[Y,X]])+\ldots\in\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbb{F}}(O),

where
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbb{F}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Sigma_{>0}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{F}}
is an orthogonal direct sum. We note that
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\mathbb{F}}
is orthogonal to the remaining terms of
\log(uu^{\prime})
, hence
X\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, see Lemma
5.5
. Thus
u=\exp(X)\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
and hence also
u^{\prime}=u^{-1}uu^{\prime}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
since
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
is a group.
∎

Proposition 5.10
.

For
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
there are
k_{\alpha}\in\Lambda\cup\{-\infty\}
for
\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}
such that

\{p\in\mathbb{A}\colon u.p\in\mathbb{A}\}=\left\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon k_{\alpha}\leq(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)\right)\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}\right\}

and therefore the set of fixed points is a finite intersection of half-apartments. If
u=u_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot u_{k}
for
u_{i}\in(U_{\alpha_{i}})_{\mathbb{F}}
with
\Sigma_{>0}=\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\}
such that
\alpha_{1}>\ldots>\alpha_{k}
, then
k_{\alpha_{i}}=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}(u_{i})
. If
u
fixes all of
\mathbb{A}
, then
u=\operatorname{Id}
.

Proof.

We use
[
App25
, Lemma
]
to write

u=u_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot u_{k}

for some
u_{i}\in(U_{\alpha_{i}})_{\mathbb{F}}
where
\Sigma_{>0}=\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\}
with
\alpha_{1}>\ldots>\alpha_{k}
. By Proposition
5.7
,
u.p\in\mathbb{A}
for
p=a.o\in\mathbb{A}
if and only if
u.p=p
and by Theorem
4.19
and
[
App25
, Proposition
]
,
a^{-1}ua\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Then

a^{-1}ua=a^{-1}u_{1}a\cdot\ldots\cdot a^{-1}u_{k}a\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

and we can apply Lemma
5.9
repeatedly to obtain
a^{-1}u_{1}a\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, …,
a^{-1}u_{k}a\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. By Proposition
5.8
, this implies

k_{\alpha_{i}}:=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}(u_{i})\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha_{i}}(a))

for all
\alpha_{i}\in\Sigma_{>0}
. All the previous implications are equivalences, concluding the description of the fixed point set of
u
. If
u
fixes all of
\mathbb{A}
, then
a^{-1}ua\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
and
a^{-1}u_{i}a\in(U_{\alpha_{i}})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. This is only possible if
\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}(u_{i})\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha_{i}}(a))
, so
u_{i}=\operatorname{Id}
for all
i
and thus
u=\operatorname{Id}
.
∎

As an application of the above, we can conclude that elements of
U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
fix the fundamental Weyl chamber, which is defined as

C_{0}:=\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon 0\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}\}.

Corollary 5.11
.

Let
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Then
u.p=p
for all
p\in C_{0}
.

Proof.

Elements
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
fix
o\in\mathcal{B}
, so by Proposition
5.10
,

o\in\{p\in\mathbb{A}\colon u.p\in\mathbb{A}\}=\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon k_{\alpha}\leq(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)\right)\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}\}

from which we conclude that
k_{\alpha}\leq 0
for all
\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}
. If now
p=a.o\in C_{0}
, then
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\geq 0\geq k_{\alpha}
, so applying Proposition
5.10
again results in
u.p=p
.
∎

We also obtain that any
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
can be conjugated by
A_{\mathbb{F}}
into
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. This statement will be useful in the proof of (A4).

Proposition 5.12
.

For every
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
there is an
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
a^{-1}ua\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Proof.

Use Proposition
5.10
to obtain
k_{\alpha}\in\Lambda\cup\{-\infty\}
for
\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}
such that
u
fixes all the points in

C:=\bigcap_{\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}}H_{\alpha,k_{\alpha}}^{+}.

We claim that
C
is non-empty. Indeed, let
c\in\mathbb{F}_{>0}
with
\lambda\coloneqq(-v)(c)>k_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha>0
.
Consider the fundamental Weyl chamber
D
as a subset of
\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Sigma^{\vee})
and let
H
be an element in the interior of
D
, in fact we may assume that for all
\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}
,
\alpha(H)\geq 1
. If we write
H=\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}q_{\delta}\delta^{\vee}\in\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Sigma^{\vee})
and let

x\coloneqq\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}q_{\delta}\delta^{\vee}\otimes\lambda\in\mathbb{A},

then

\displaystyle\alpha(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}q_{\delta}\alpha(\delta^{\vee})\lambda=\lambda\alpha\left(\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}q_{\delta}\delta^{\vee}\right)=\lambda\alpha(H)\geq\lambda,

see Sections
2.2
and
2.3
for definitions. Using the identification
\mathbb{A}\cong A_{\Lambda}
from Theorem
4.12
,
x\in\mathbb{A}
corresponds to
[a]\in A_{\Lambda}
with
\overline{\chi}_{\alpha}([a])\geq\lambda
for all
\alpha>0
, see Theorem
4.13
. Any representative
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
of
[a]\in A_{\Lambda}
satisfies
(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}(a)\right)\geq\lambda>k_{\alpha}
, so
a.o\in C
. By
[
App25
, Proposition
]
,
a^{-1}ua\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
and by Theorem
4.19
,
a^{-1}ua.o=a^{-1}a.o=o
implies
a^{-1}ua\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.
∎

5.4.
Stabilizers of apartment, half-apartments and Weyl-chambers

In Corollary
5.11
we proved that
U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
fixes the fundamental Weyl chamber
C_{0}=\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon\chi_{\alpha}(a)\geq 1\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Delta\}
, where
\Delta
is a basis of
\Sigma
. The goal of this subsection is to describe the whole stabilizer of
C_{0}
using the group
B_{\mathbb{F}}=U_{\mathbb{F}}A_{\mathbb{F}}M_{\mathbb{F}}
from
[
App25
, Section
]
. So far, we considered the action of
U_{\mathbb{F}}
and
A_{\mathbb{F}}
, now we continue by investigating the action of
K_{\mathbb{F}}
.

To determine which elements of
K_{\mathbb{F}}
fix the standard apartment
\mathbb{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
, we will use some CAT(0) geometry on the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}}
, equipped with the right metric.

Theorem 5.13

(Proposition II.2.2
[
BH99
]
)
.

If
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
are two unit-speed geodesics in a CAT(0)-space, then the function

t\mapsto d(\gamma(t),\gamma^{\prime}(t))

is convex.

In the non-standard symmetric space
\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}
, the elements of
K_{\mathbb{F}}
that fix all points of the standard maximal flat
A_{\mathbb{F}}.\operatorname{Id}
are exactly
\operatorname{Cen}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})=:M_{\mathbb{F}}
. In the next Proposition we show that these are also exactly the elements in
K_{\mathbb{F}}
that fix
\mathbb{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
pointwise.

Proposition 5.14
.

Elements
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
fix
\mathbb{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
pointwise if and only if
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

Recall that the distance on the non-standard symmetric space
\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}
is given by
d=(-v)\circ N\circ\delta\colon\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{F}}\to\Lambda
. We claim that the first-order formula

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\quad\forall k\in K\colon

\displaystyle(\forall a\in A\colon a.\operatorname{Id}=k.a.\operatorname{Id})

\displaystyle\vee(\forall c>0\ \exists a\in A\colon N(\delta(a^{\prime}.\operatorname{Id},k.a.\operatorname{Id}))>c)

holds over
\mathbb{F}
. The situation is illustrated in Figure
5
.
Informally,
\varphi
states that
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
either fixes all points in the maximal flat
A_{\mathbb{F}}.\operatorname{Id}
, or there are points
a.\operatorname{Id}
that are sent arbitrarily far away by
k
. To prove that
\varphi
holds over
\mathbb{F}
, it suffices to show
\varphi
over
\mathbb{R}
by the transfer principle.

Figure 5
.
The first-order formula
\varphi
states that either
k
fixes all points in
A_{\mathbb{F}}
or there are points
a.\operatorname{Id},k.a.\operatorname{Id}
whose distance is arbitrarily large.

We note that changing the
W_{s}
-invariant multiplicative norm
N
only changes
\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}}
up to quasi-isometry, so we may choose
N_{\mathbb{R}}
coming from a scalar product, even if
N_{\mathbb{R}}
is then not semialgebraic, as the truth of
\varphi
only depends on
N_{\mathbb{R}}
up to equivalency. So consider

\displaystyle N_{\mathbb{R}}\colon A_{\mathbb{R}}.\operatorname{Id}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}

\displaystyle a.\operatorname{Id}

\displaystyle\mapsto\exp\left(\sqrt{B_{\theta}(\log(a),\log(a))}\right)

for the scalar product
B_{\theta}
on
\mathfrak{a}
. Then by the general theory of symmetric spaces of non-compact type,
\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}}
with the distance
d=\log\circ N_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\delta_{\mathbb{R}}
is a complete CAT(0)-space.
Consider a unit-speed geodesic
\gamma\colon\mathbb{R}\to A_{\mathbb{R}}.\operatorname{Id}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}}
passing through
\gamma(0)=\operatorname{Id}
. Then
k.\gamma
is also a unit-speed geodesic passing through
\operatorname{Id}
. By Theorem
5.13
, the function
f\colon t\mapsto d(\gamma(t),k.\gamma(t))
is convex. Since
f
is non-negative and
f(0)=0
,
f
then has to be constant (hence
a.\operatorname{Id}=k.a.\operatorname{Id}
for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
) or eventually be larger than
\log(c)
for every
c\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
(hence there is some
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
N_{\mathbb{R}}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}}(a.\operatorname{Id},k.a.\operatorname{Id}))>c
).

Now that
\varphi
is established over
\mathbb{F}
, we consider some
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
that fixes all points of
\mathbb{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
. Choosing
c\in\mathbb{F}_{>0}
with
c\notin O
, we see that the second option in
\varphi
cannot be true, whence
k.a.\operatorname{Id}=a.\operatorname{Id}
for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, or equivalently
k\tilde{a}k{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}=\tilde{a}
for all
\tilde{a}=a^{2}\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. This means
k\in\operatorname{Cen}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
using
k{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}=k^{-1}
.
∎

In Proposition
5.16
, we strengthen the previous result by only requiring
k
to fix a chamber of
\mathbb{A}
. We first need a preliminary result.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
and
a,b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
k.a.o=a.o
, then
k.a^{-1}.o=a^{-1}.o
. If moreover
k.b.o=b.o
, then
k.\sqrt{ab}.o=\sqrt{ab}.o
.

Proof.

We first assume that
A_{\mathbb{F}}
consists of diagonal matrices, so we may write
a=\operatorname{Diag}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
and
b=\operatorname{Diag}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
. Then

\displaystyle k.a.o=a.o

\displaystyle\iff a^{-1}ka\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\iff\forall i,j\colon k_{ij}\frac{a_{j}}{a_{i}}\in O

\displaystyle\iff\forall j,i\colon k{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}_{ij}\frac{a_{i}}{a_{j}}\in O\iff ak{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}a^{-1}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

\displaystyle\iff k{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}.a^{-1}.o=a^{-1}.o\iff a^{-1}.o=k.a^{-1}.o.

Moreover, if
k.a.o=a.o
and
k.b.o=b.o
, then
k_{ij}a_{j}/a_{i}\cdot k_{ij}b_{j}/b_{i}\in O
for all
i,j
. Then also

k_{ij}\frac{\sqrt{a_{j}b_{j}}}{\sqrt{a_{i}b_{i}}}\in O

for all
i,j
, which translates to
k.\sqrt{ab}.o=\sqrt{ab}.o
.

In general, the matrices in
A_{\mathbb{F}}
may not be diagonal, but they are symmetric. By the spectral theorem for symmetric matrices, which holds over
\mathbb{F}
by the transfer principle,
A_{\mathbb{F}}
is orthogonally diagonalizable, meaning that there is some
Q\in\operatorname{SO}(n)
such that
QA_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}
is diagonal. We can then apply the above arguments to the group
QG_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}<\operatorname{SL}_{n}(\mathbb{F})
. For
k.a.o=a.o
we obtain

\displaystyle a^{-1}ka\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

\displaystyle\iff(QaQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})^{-1}QkQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}QaQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}\in(QG_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})(O)

\displaystyle\iff QaQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}Qk{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}(QaQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})^{-1}\in(QG_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})(O)

\displaystyle\iff ak{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}a^{-1}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)

and complete the argument as above. When additionally
b^{-1}kb\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
we have

\displaystyle(QbQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})^{-1}QkQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}QbQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}\in(QG_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})(O)

which by the above implies

\displaystyle(Q\sqrt{ab}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})^{-1}QkQ{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}Q\sqrt{ab}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}\in(QG_{\mathbb{F}}Q{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!})(O)

and thus
\sqrt{ab}^{-1}k\sqrt{ab}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O).

∎

Let
C_{0}=\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon\chi_{\alpha}(a)\geq 1\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Delta\}
be the fundamental Weyl chamber associated to a basis
\Delta
of
\Sigma
.

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
k.p=p
for all
p\in C_{0}
. Then
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
and hence
k
fixes all points in
\mathbb{A}
. In fact, if
k
fixes all the points in
a.C_{0}
for any
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, then
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

We first claim that every element
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
is of the form
a=a_{1}a_{2}^{-1}
for
a_{1}.o,a_{2}.o\in C_{0}
. To see this, we show that the first-order formula

\varphi\colon\quad\quad\forall a\in A\colon\exists a_{1},a_{2}\in A\colon a=a_{1}\cdot a_{2}^{-1}\wedge\bigwedge_{\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}}\chi_{\alpha}(a_{1})\geq 1\wedge\chi_{\alpha}(a_{2})\geq 1

holds over
\mathbb{R}
and then apply the transfer principle. Over
\mathbb{R}
we can transfer the problem to the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbb{R}}
using the logarithm. We equip
\mathfrak{a}
with the distance defined by the scalar product
B_{\theta}
.
Let
H:=\log(a)
and
R:=\sqrt{B_{\theta}(H,H)}
. Since
\mathfrak{c}_{0}:=\{H\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\alpha(H)>0\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Sigma_{>0}\}
contains an open cone, it contains a ball
B_{r}(H^{\prime})
for some
r>0
and
H^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{c}_{0}
. Scaling the ball by the factor
R/r
, we obtain that
B_{R}(R/r\cdot H^{\prime})\subseteq\mathfrak{c}_{0}
. As in Figure
6
, we define
H_{1}=R/r\cdot H^{\prime}
and
H_{2}=H_{1}-H
which lies on the boundary of
B_{R}(R/r\cdot H^{\prime})
and hence also in
\mathfrak{c}_{0}
. Then
a=\exp(H_{1})\exp(H_{2})^{-1}
, concluding the proof of
\varphi
over
\mathbb{R}
and hence over
\mathbb{F}
.

Figure 6
.
We use that the cone
\mathfrak{c}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbb{R}}
contains an open ball, which we can scale to obtain
H_{1},H_{2}\in\mathfrak{c}_{0}
with
H=H_{1}-H_{2}
.

If now
k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
fixes
C_{0}
pointwise and
p=a.o\in\mathbb{A}
. Then
a=a_{1}a_{2}^{-1}
as above with
a_{1}.o,a_{2}.o\in C_{0}
. Since
C_{0}
is a cone, also
a_{1}^{2}.o,a_{2}^{2}.o\in C_{0}
, so
k.a_{1}^{2}.o=a_{1}^{2}.o
and
k.a_{2}^{2}.o=a_{2}^{2}.o
. By Lemma
5.15
, then
k.a_{2}^{-2}.o=a_{2}^{-2}.o
and

k.a.o=k.\sqrt{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}^{-2}}.o=\sqrt{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}^{-2}}.o=a.o,

completing the first statement of the proof. If
k
fixes
a.C_{0}
for some
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, a modification of the above argument similarly implies that
k
fixes all of
\mathbb{A}
pointwise.
∎

Theorem 5.17
.

The pointwise stabilizer of
C_{0}
in
G_{\mathbb{F}}
is

B_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}.

Proof.

If
g=uak\in B_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
and
p\in C_{0}
, then
g.p=uak.p=ua.p=u.p=p
, where the last equality follows from Corollary
5.11
.

If
g
fixes
C_{0}
pointwise, in particular it fixes
o\in C_{0}
, hence
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, which can be decomposed to
G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)K_{\mathbb{F}}
by Corollary
4.20
. Therefore
g=uak
with
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Therefore we have that
k
fixes
C_{0}
pointwise. Proposition
5.16
now concludes the proof by showing
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
.
∎

Recall that
S<G
is a maximal
\mathbb{K}
-split torus and
A_{\mathbb{F}}<S_{\mathbb{F}}
is the semialgebraically connected component of the identity. In the following we consider the groups
T_{\mathbb{F}}:=\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
and
T_{\mathbb{F}}(O):=T_{\mathbb{F}}\cap G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Sometimes (namely when
G
is quasi-split),
T=\operatorname{Cen}_{G}(S)
is a maximal algebraic torus, but we do not use this in what follows.

Lemma 5.18
.

We have
T_{\mathbb{F}}=\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})=M_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot A_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

The inclusion
\supseteq
is clear. For the other direction, let
g\in T_{\mathbb{F}}
and choose an Iwasawa decomposition
g=nak
with
n\in U_{\mathbb{F}},a\in A_{\mathbb{F}},k\in K_{\mathbb{F}}
, see
[
App25
, Theorem
]
. For all
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
we have
nak.b.o=b.nak.o=bna.o
, and thus
k.b.o=a^{-1}n^{-1}bna.o
. Denoting
\tilde{n}:=(a^{-1}n^{-1}a)(a^{-1}bnb^{-1}a)\in U_{\mathbb{F}}
, where we made use of
[
App25
, Proposition
]
. Then
k.b.o=\tilde{n}.b.o
for all
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. By Proposition
5.12
there is a
c\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
\tilde{\tilde{n}}:=c\tilde{n}c^{-1}\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
and hence
\tilde{\tilde{n}}
fixes the elements of
C_{0}
by Theorem
5.17
. In particular,
\tilde{n}
fixes
c.C_{0}
and thus
k
fixes
c.C_{0}
, since
k.p=\tilde{n}.p
for all
p\in\mathbb{A}
. By Lemma
5.16
, we then have
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
. This implies also
\tilde{n}.p=p
for all
p\in\mathbb{A}
, hence
\tilde{n}=\operatorname{Id}
. Then
bnb^{-1}=n
for all
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
, not only the ones with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=0
, hence by
[
App25
, Lemma
]
,
n=\operatorname{Id}
. Thus
g=ak
with
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.19
.

The pointwise stabilizer of
\mathbb{A}
in
G_{\mathbb{F}}
is
T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

Elements of
A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
fix all points in
\mathbb{A}
. If
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
fixes all points of
\mathbb{A}
, it fixes in particular the points in
C_{0}
, so
g=uak
with
u\in U_{\mathbb{F}}(O),a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O),k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
by Theorem
5.17
. By the description of the stabilizer of
u
in Proposition
5.10
,
u
can only fix all of
\mathbb{A}
if
u=\operatorname{Id}
, so
g\in A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
. By Lemma
5.18
,
T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.20
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma
. The pointwise stabilizer of the half-apartment

H_{\alpha}^{+}=\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\geq 0\}

in
G_{\mathbb{F}}
is
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\Sigma
is equipped with an order in which
\alpha>0
. Then
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(H_{\alpha}^{+})
by Proposition
5.8
. If
g\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(H_{\alpha}^{+})
, then
g=uak\in U_{\alpha}(O)A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
by Theorem
5.17
since
C_{0}\subseteq H_{\alpha}
, so in fact
u
fixes
H_{\alpha}^{+}
pointwise. By
[
App25
, Lemma
]
,
u=u_{1}\cdots u_{k}
where
u_{i}\in U_{\alpha_{i}}
such that
\alpha_{1}>\ldots>\alpha_{k}>0
. Then we can apply the refined version of Proposition
5.10
, to obtain

\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{A}}(u)=\left\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon k_{\alpha_{i}}\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha_{i}}(a))\text{ for all }\alpha_{i}\in\Sigma_{>0}\right\}

where
k_{\alpha_{i}}=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}(u_{i})
. In our case,
H_{\alpha}^{+}\subseteq\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{A}}(u)
and since when
k_{\alpha_{i}}\neq-\infty

H_{\alpha}^{+}\subseteq\left\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon k_{\alpha_{i}}\leq(-v)(\chi_{\alpha_{i}}(a))\right\}

implies
\alpha=\alpha_{i}
,
k_{\alpha_{i}}=-\infty
for all
\alpha_{i}\neq\alpha
and
k_{\alpha}=0
, so
\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}(u_{i})=-\infty
implies
u_{i}=\operatorname{Id}
whenever
\alpha_{i}\neq\alpha
, leading to
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
concluding the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.21
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma
,
\ell\in\Lambda
. The pointwise stabilizer of the affine half-apartment

H_{\alpha,\ell}^{+}=\left\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\geq\ell\right\}

in
G_{\mathbb{F}}
is
U_{\alpha,\ell}A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
, where
U_{\alpha,\ell}=\{u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\colon u\text{ stabilizes }H_{\alpha,\ell}\text{ pointwise}\}
.

Proof.

If
g\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
stabilizes
H_{\alpha,\ell}^{+}
pointwise and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
satisfies
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=\ell
, then
a^{-1}ga\in G_{\mathbb{F}}
stabilizes
H_{\alpha,\ell}^{+}
pointwise. By Theorem
5.20
and since
A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
, we have
g\in a(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)a^{-1}A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)M_{\mathbb{F}}
. While elements
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
stabilize elements
b.o
with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=0
pointwise,
aua^{-1}
stabilize elements
ab.o
with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(ab))=\ell+0
pointwise, concluding the proof.
∎

5.5.
Bruhat-Tits theory for root groups

The goal of Subsections
5.5
,
5.6
and
5.7
is to describe the pointwise stabilizer of a finite subset
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{A}
in Theorem
5.45
. To obtain this, we first consider points fixed by
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
in Section
5.5
, then points fixed by the rank one subgroups generated by
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
in Section
5.6
, before taking on the whole group
G_{\mathbb{F}}
in
5.7
. These subsections are inspired by the study of stabilizers in
[
BT72
, Sections 6 and 7]
, for a good English reference see
[
Lan96
]
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{A}
be any subset of the apartment
\mathbb{A}
. Let

\displaystyle U_{\alpha,\Omega}

\displaystyle:=\left\{u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\colon u.p=p\text{ for all }p\in\Omega\right\}

denote the pointwise stabilizer of
\Omega
in the group
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. The subscript
\mathbb{F}
is no longer needed, since there is no corresponding group of
\mathbb{R}
-points. In view of Proposition
5.7
, Proposition
5.8
can be reformulated.

Lemma 5.22
.

For roots
\alpha\in\Sigma
and any subset
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{A}
, we have

\displaystyle U_{\alpha,\Omega}

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\colon\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq(-v)\left(\chi_{\alpha}\left(a\right)\right)\text{ for all }a.o\in\Omega\right\}.

For any
\ell\in\Lambda
, denote

U_{\alpha,\ell}:=\left\{u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\colon\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq\ell\right\}.

Note that if
\ell=\min_{a.o\in\Omega}\{(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\}
and
\alpha\in\Sigma
, then Lemma
5.22
can be reformulated as

U_{\alpha,\Omega}=U_{\alpha,\ell}.

In the setting of
[
BT72
]
,
\ell=\inf_{a.o\in\Omega}\{(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\}
with
U_{\alpha,\Omega}=U_{\alpha,\ell}
always exists, since they work with
\Lambda=\mathbb{R}
. In our case we have to be more careful. If
\Omega
is a finite set, this
\ell
exists. In particular, when
|\Omega|=1
, we have for any
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}

U_{\alpha,\left\{a.o\right\}}=U_{\alpha,(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))}.

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma,\ell\in\Lambda
and
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. Then

aU_{\alpha,\ell}a^{-1}=U_{\alpha,\ell+(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))}.

Proof.

Let
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=\ell
, the existence of which can be concluded from
[
App25
, Lemma
]
. We consider the single element set
\Omega=\{b.o\}
. Then
U_{\alpha,\ell}=U_{\alpha,\Omega}
. Let
u\in U_{\alpha,\ell}
. Then
aua^{-1}\in U_{\alpha,a.\Omega}
since

aua^{-1}.(a.b.o)=au.b.o=a.b.o

and since
aua^{-1}\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
, see
[
App25
, Proposition
]
. Now since

(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(ab))=(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))+(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=\ell+(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))

we have
aU_{\alpha,\ell}a^{-1}=aU_{\alpha,\Omega}a^{-1}=U_{\alpha,a.\Omega}=U_{\alpha,\ell+(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))}
.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma,\ell\in\Lambda
and
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}=\operatorname{Cen}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
. Then

kU_{\alpha,\ell}k^{-1}=U_{\alpha,\ell}.

Proof.

Since
k\in M_{\mathbb{F}}
, it represents the trivial element of the spherical Weyl group acting on the root system. In particular
kU_{\alpha}k^{-1}=U_{\alpha}
.

Let
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=\ell
and
\Omega=\{b.o\}
. Then
U_{\alpha,\ell}=U_{\alpha,\Omega}
. For any
u\in U_{\alpha,\ell}
we have

kuk^{-1}.b.o=ku.b.o=k.b.o=b.o,

hence
kU_{\alpha,\Omega}k^{-1}=U_{\alpha,\Omega}
concluding the proof.
∎

Putting the previous two results together shows that
U_{\alpha,\ell}
is invariant under conjugation by elements in the pointwise stabilizer
T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Lemma 5.25
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma,\ell\in\Lambda
and
t\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=M_{\mathbb{F}}A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Then

tU_{\alpha,\ell}t^{-1}=U_{\alpha,\ell}.

5.6.
Bruhat-Tits theory in rank 1

Our goal in this section is to study the group generated by
U_{\alpha,\Omega}
and
U_{-\alpha,\Omega}
. For this we will use Jacobson-Morozov in the form of
[
App25
, Proposition
]
. Therefore we have to restrict ourselves to reduced root systems from now on. In this subsection we fix
\alpha\in\Sigma
such that
(\mathfrak{g}_{2\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=0
and
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
.

When
u\neq\operatorname{Id}
, there is a
t\in\mathbb{F}
and an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triplet
(X,Y,H)
as in
[
App25
, Proposition
]

u=\exp(tX)
. Up to choosing a different
t\in\mathbb{F}
we may assume
(-v)(X)=0
, in which case also
(-v)(Y)=0
since
\exp(tY)=\exp(tX)^{{}^{\mathsf{T}}\!}
, and
(-v)(H)=0
, as
H=[X,Y]
. Moreover, there is an algebraic group homomorphism
\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\colon\operatorname{SL}(2,\mathbb{F})\to G_{\mathbb{F}}
with finite kernel such that

\displaystyle u=\exp(tX)=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\exp(tY)=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
t&1\end{pmatrix}.

We note that
t\in O
if and only if
u\in G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, using Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 5.26
.

For every
t\in\mathbb{F}
we have

\varphi_{\alpha}\left(\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right)=(-v)(t)=\varphi_{-\alpha}\left(\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
t&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

Proof.

Since
(-v)(X):=\max_{ij}\{(-v)(X_{ij})\}=0
,
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=(-v)(tX)=(-v)(t)
. Similarly
\varphi_{-\alpha}(\exp(tY))=(-v)(tY)=(-v)(t)
.
∎

Lemma 5.27
.

Let
\ell:=(-v)(t)
. Then

m(u):=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}0&t\\
-1/t&0\end{pmatrix}=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1/t&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1/t&1\end{pmatrix},\right)

in particular
m(u)\in U_{-\alpha,-\ell}U_{\alpha,\ell}U_{-\alpha,-\ell}
.

Proof.

Let

u^{\prime}:=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1/t&1\end{pmatrix}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}.

The matrix expression
m(u)=u^{\prime}uu^{\prime}
is a direct calculation, showing
m(u)\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. By Lemma
5.26
,
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\ell
and
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=(-v)(-1/t)=-\ell
, concluding the proof.
∎

The element

m(u):=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}0&t\\
-1/t&0\end{pmatrix}\in G_{\mathbb{F}}

is contained in
\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
by
[
App25
, Lemma
]
and thus a representative of an element of the affine Weyl group
W_{a}=\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})/\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
. Recall that the affine Weyl group
W_{a}
can be identified with
W_{a}=\mathbb{A}\rtimes W_{s}
, where
W_{s}=N_{\mathbb{F}}/M_{\mathbb{F}}=\operatorname{Nor}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})/\operatorname{Cen}_{K_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
is the spherical Weyl group.

Proposition 5.28
.

The action of
m(u)
decomposes as

m(u)=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&t^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}=:a_{t}\cdot m\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot N_{\mathbb{F}}

into an affine part represented by
a_{t}
and a spherical part represented by
m
.
The element
m
represents the reflection
r_{\alpha}\in W_{s}
and for
a_{t}
we have
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a_{t}))=2(-v)(t)=2\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
. Thus
m(u)
represents the affine reflection along the hyperplane

\{a.o\in\mathbb{A}\colon(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\}.

Proof.

The decomposition of
m(u)
is a direct calculation. We investigate the action of
m
. We may decompose
A_{\mathbb{F}}=(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot A_{\perp}
as a direct product, where

(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\left(\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&0\\
0&\lambda^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\colon\lambda>0\right\}\right)

and

A_{\perp}=\{a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\colon\chi_{\alpha}(a)=1\}.

The reflection
r_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}
is defined by
r_{\alpha}(a_{\alpha}.o)=a_{\alpha}^{-1}.o
for all
a_{\alpha}\in(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
r_{\alpha}(a_{\perp}.o)=a_{\perp}.o
for all
a_{\perp}\in A_{\perp}
. For
a_{\alpha}\in(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
there is some
\lambda>0
such that

\displaystyle m.a_{\alpha}.o

\displaystyle=m.a_{\alpha}.m^{-1}.o=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&0\\
0&\lambda^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\right).o

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}\lambda^{-1}&0\\
0&\lambda\end{pmatrix}.o=a_{\alpha}^{-1}.o.

For
a_{\perp}\in A_{\perp}
we use

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\in\exp((\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot\exp((\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot\exp((\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})

and
[
App25
, Lemma
]
to obtain
m.a_{\perp}.o=a_{\perp}.m.o=a_{\perp}.o
. By
[
App25
, Lemma
]
and Lemma
5.26
,
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a_{t}))=2(-v)(t)=2\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
. A point
a.o=a_{\perp}a_{\alpha}.o\in\mathbb{A}
is fixed by
m(u)
if and only if

a.o=m(u).a.o=a_{t}ma_{\perp}a_{\alpha}m^{-1}.o=a_{t}a_{\perp}a_{\alpha}^{-1}.o

which is the case exactly when
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a_{t}a_{\alpha}^{-2}))=0
, i.e. when
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=(-v)(t)=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
.
∎

We obtain three corollaries from the geometric description above. Recall from Theorem
5.19
that
\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(\mathbb{A})=M_{\mathbb{F}}A_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Lemma 5.29
.

For any
u_{1},u_{2}\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
,

\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{2})\quad\iff\quad m(u_{2})^{-1}m(u_{1})\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O).

For any
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},u^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
,

\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=-\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})\quad\iff\quad m(u)^{-1}m(u^{\prime})\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O).

Proof.

We use the description of the action in Proposition
5.28
. Both
m(u_{1})
and
m(u_{2})
act on
\mathbb{A}
by an affine reflection along a hyperplane. They reflect along the same hyperplane if and only if
\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{2})
, which is the case exactly when
m(u_{2})^{-1}m(u_{1})\in\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(\mathbb{A})
.

The fixed hyperplanes of
m(u)
and
m(u^{\prime})
are given respectively by the conditions
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
and
(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(a))=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})
. The second condition can also be written as
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=-\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
and hence agrees with the first.
∎

Lemma 5.30
.

We have
m(u)\cdot M_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot m(u)^{-1}=M_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

Let
m(u)=a_{t}\cdot m\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot N_{\mathbb{F}}
as in Proposition
5.28
. In the spherical Weyl group
W_{s}=N_{\mathbb{F}}/M_{\mathbb{F}}
we have
mM_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot m^{-1}M_{\mathbb{F}}=M_{\mathbb{F}}
. Then

m(u)\cdot M_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot m(u)^{-1}=a_{t}mM_{\mathbb{F}}m^{-1}a_{t}^{-1}=a_{t}M_{\mathbb{F}}a_{t}^{-1}=M_{\mathbb{F}}.\qed

Lemma 5.31
.

Let

a,b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
a.o=m(u).b.o
, then

(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=(-v)(t^{2}\chi_{\alpha}(b)^{-1}).

Moreover,

m(u)U_{\alpha,\ell}m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,\ell-2(-v)(t)}=U_{-\alpha,\ell-2\varphi_{\alpha}(u)}

for any
\ell\in\Lambda
.

Proof.

Decomposing
m(u)=a_{t}\cdot m\in A_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot N_{\mathbb{F}}
as in Proposition
5.28
, we see
m(u)
as consisting of a translational element
a_{t}
and a representative of an element
w=[m]
of the spherical Weyl group
W_{s}
.

If
a,b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
satisfy
a.o=m(u).b.o=a_{t}mbm^{-1}.o
, then

\displaystyle(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))

\displaystyle=(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a_{t}mbm^{-1}))=(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a_{t})\chi_{\alpha}(mbm^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(-v)(t^{2}\chi_{\alpha}(b)^{-1}).

where the last equality comes from
[
App25
, Lemma
and
]
.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.23
, we now consider
\Omega=\{b.o\}
with
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))=\ell
. Then
U_{\alpha,\ell}=U_{\alpha,\Omega}
. By
[
App25
, Lemma
and
]
, we have

\displaystyle m(u)\cdot(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot m(u)^{-1}

\displaystyle=a_{t}m(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}m^{-1}a_{t}^{-1}=a_{t}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}a_{t}^{-1}=(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}.

Since

(m(u)\cdot u\cdot m(u)^{-1}).m(u).b.o=m(u).b.o,

we have thus
m(u)\cdot U_{\alpha,\Omega}\cdot m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,m(u).\Omega}
.
Now by the comment after Lemma
5.22
,
U_{-\alpha,m(u).\Omega}=U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
for
\ell^{\prime}=(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(a))
where
a.o=m(u).b.o
. We have

\ell^{\prime}=(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(a))=-(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(b))-2(-v)(t)=\ell-2(-v)(t)

whence
m(u)U_{\alpha,\ell}m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,\ell-2(-v)(t)}
.
∎

We know from Lemma
5.27
, that
m(u)\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. Next, we will show that
m(u)
is the only element in
\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Lemma 5.32
.

Let

u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
. Then
u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}=m(u)
and

\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=-\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime\prime}).

Proof.

Recall that if

u=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{u}:=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1/t&1\end{pmatrix},

then

\displaystyle m(u)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1/t&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1/t&1\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{u}^{-1}u\overline{u}^{-1}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}.

Then
u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}=u^{\prime}\overline{u}\cdot m(u)\cdot\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
and
\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. Now for any
\ell\leq-\varphi_{-\alpha}(\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime})
, use
[
App25
, Lemma
]
to obtain
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))=\ell
or equivalently
(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(a))=-\ell
. By Lemma
5.22
this means that
\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}\in U_{-\alpha,-\ell}=U_{-\alpha,\{a.o\}}
. Let
b\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
be such that

b.o=u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}.a.o=u^{\prime}\overline{u}\cdot m(u)\cdot\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}.a.o=u^{\prime}\overline{u}\cdot m(u)\cdot a.o

and applying Proposition
5.7
,
b.o=m(u).a.o
. Since

(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(b))=(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))-2(-v)(t)=\ell-2(-v)(t)

by Lemma
5.31
, we can use Lemma
5.22
to obtain
u^{\prime}\overline{u}\in U_{-\alpha,\{b.o\}}=U_{-\alpha,\ell-2(-v)(t)}
for all
\ell\leq-\varphi_{-\alpha}(\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime})
. Therefore
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime}\overline{u})\leq\lambda
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
. The only element of
(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
with this property is
u^{\prime}\overline{u}=\operatorname{Id}
.

Now for all
a\in A_{\mathbb{F}}
,

\displaystyle u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}.a.o

\displaystyle=u^{\prime}\overline{u}m(u)\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}.a.o=m(u).\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}.a.o\in\mathbb{A}

and thus
\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}.a.o\in\mathbb{A}
, hence
\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}.a.o=a.o
by Proposition
5.7
. The only element of
(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
acting trivially on all of
\mathbb{A}
is the identity, so
\overline{u}u^{\prime\prime}=\operatorname{Id}
. Thus
u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}=\overline{u}^{-1}u\overline{u}^{-1}=m(u)
. Moreover

\displaystyle\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime\prime})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(\overline{u}^{-1})=(-v)(-1/t)=-(-v)(t)=-\varphi_{\alpha}(u).\qed

Figure 7
.
Illustration of the action of elements
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},u^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
with
\ell:=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
and
\ell^{\prime}:=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})
in the case where
\ell+\ell^{\prime}<0
(left),
\ell=-\ell^{\prime}
(middle) and
\ell+\ell^{\prime}>0
. An element
m(u)
of the affine Weyl group can only be generated by
u,u^{\prime}
in the case
\ell=-\ell^{\prime}
.

The actions of
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},u^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
depending on their root group valuations
\ell,\ell^{\prime}
are illustrated in Figure
7
. Only when
\ell+\ell^{\prime}\leq 0
do
u,u^{\prime}
fix a common point. To study the stabilizer we consider the cases
\ell+\ell^{\prime}<0
and
\ell=-\ell^{\prime}
separately.

Lemma 5.33
.

Let

u^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)+\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})<0
. Then there is an element
(u_{1},t,u_{1}^{\prime})\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\times T_{\mathbb{F}}\times(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
u^{\prime}u=u_{1}tu_{1}^{\prime}
.

Moreover
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})
,
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{1}^{\prime})
and
t\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.

Proof.

If
u=\operatorname{Id}
we can choose
u_{1}=\operatorname{Id},t=\operatorname{Id}
and
u_{1}^{\prime}=u^{\prime}
. We may thus assume
u\neq\operatorname{Id}
. The element
u^{\prime}u
lies in the group
(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
defined in
[
App25
, Section
]
. As
(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
is again a semisimple liear algebraic group, subgroups
(K_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
,
(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
can be identified as we did for
G_{\mathbb{F}}
in Section
4
. By
[
App25
, Corollary
]
,

(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(B_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\ \amalg\ m\cdot(B_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},

where
(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}:=(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
(B_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}:=(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. We note that by
[
App25
, Lemma
]
and then Lemma
5.18
that
(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq M_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot A_{\mathbb{F}}=T_{\mathbb{F}}
.

We have that
u^{\prime}u\notin m\cdot(B_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
, since otherwise there exists
u^{\prime\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
such that

u^{\prime}uu^{\prime\prime}\in m\cdot(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})

and hence by Lemma
5.32
,
-\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})
contradicting our assumption. Thus

\displaystyle u^{\prime}u

\displaystyle\in(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(B_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot T_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}.

Let now
u_{1}\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},\ u_{1}^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
t\in(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
with
u^{\prime}u=u_{1}tu_{1}^{\prime}
.
If
u_{1}=\operatorname{Id}
,
u=(u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{1}tu_{1}^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}M_{\mathbb{F}}A_{\mathbb{F}}\cap U_{\alpha}
, but since
u
can not send some halfapartment
H_{-\alpha,\ell}^{+}
to a halfapartment
H_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}^{+}
and at the same times fix a halfapartment
H_{\alpha,k}^{+}
, see Proposition
5.8
and Corollary
5.21
,
u=\operatorname{Id}
and we can argue as in the beginning of the proof. Thus we may assume that
u_{1}\neq\operatorname{Id}
. We now show
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})
. Consider the element
m(u_{1})\in\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
. Then

m(u_{1})\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u_{1}(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},

so let
u_{2}^{\prime},u_{3}^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
u_{1}=u_{2}^{\prime}m(u_{1})u_{3}^{\prime}
and by Lemma
5.32

\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{2}^{\prime})=-\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{3}^{\prime})
. Then

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=(u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{1}tu_{1}^{\prime}=(u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime}m(u_{1})u_{3}^{\prime}tu_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=((u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime})m(u_{1})t(t^{-1}u_{3}^{\prime}tu_{1}^{\prime})\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}m(u_{1})t(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

where we used that
t^{-1}u_{3}^{\prime}t\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
by
[
App25
, Proposition
]
and the fact that
(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
acts trivially on the rank 1 root system
\{\alpha,-\alpha\}
. Applying Lemma
5.32
again gives
\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime})=-\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
. Now since
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)+\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})<0
, we have

\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})<-\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime})\leq\max\{\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1}),\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{2}^{\prime})\}

from which we can infer that
\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1})\neq\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{2}^{\prime})
and thus by the second part of Lemma
5.4
,
\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{2}^{\prime})
, whence

\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=-\varphi_{-\alpha}((u^{\prime})^{-1}u_{2}^{\prime})=-\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{2}^{\prime})=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1}).

Proving
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u_{1}^{\prime})
works the same way, after replacing
u
by
u^{\prime}
,
u_{1}
by
u_{1}^{\prime}
and
\alpha
by
-\alpha
.

From the above discussion, we obtain

m(u_{1})t=((u_{2}^{\prime})^{-1}u^{\prime})u((u_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}t^{-1}(u_{3}^{\prime})^{-1}t)\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

which by the uniqueness statement of Lemma
5.32
implies
m(u_{1})t=m(u)
. Since
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})
, we know by Proposition
5.28
that both
m(u_{1})
and
m(u)
represent the same element of the affine Weyl group: the reflection
r_{\alpha}
followed by a translation of length
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)=\varphi_{\alpha}(u_{1})
. This means that
[t]=[m(u_{1})^{-1}][m(u)]=[\operatorname{Id}]\in W_{a}
, in particular
t
fixes
o
, so
t\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
.
∎

Proposition 5.34
.

Let

\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
\ell+\ell^{\prime}<0
. Let

L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=\langle u\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cup U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\rangle

and
H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\cap T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Then

L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}=U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}.

Proof.

The inclusion
\supseteq
is clear. We show the other direction by induction on the word length. If the length of a word in
L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
is
1
, then it lies in
U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
. It remains to show that for every
u_{\pm\alpha}\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cup U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
, and
v=u\cdot u^{\prime}\cdot h\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
, we have
u_{\pm\alpha}v\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
. If
u_{\alpha}\in U_{\alpha,\ell}
, this is straightforward, so let
u_{-\alpha}\in U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
5.33
, we can find
(u_{1},t_{1},u_{1}^{\prime})\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\times T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\times U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
such that
u_{-\alpha}u=u_{1}t_{1}u_{1}^{\prime}
. Then
u_{-\alpha}v=u_{1}t_{1}u_{1}^{\prime}u^{\prime}h
. By Lemma
5.25
,
t_{1}U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}t_{1}^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
, so
u_{-\alpha}v\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
as required.
∎

Next up, we consider the situation
\ell+\ell^{\prime}=0
. For this we introduce

U_{-\alpha,-\ell+}:=\bigcup_{t<-\ell}U_{-\alpha,t}\subseteq U_{-\alpha,-\ell}

which is a group by Lemma
5.4
.

Proposition 5.35
.

Let

\ell:=\varphi_{\alpha}(u)
and let
m(u)
as in
[
App25
, Lemma
]
. Let

L_{\ell,-\ell}:=\langle\tilde{u}\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cup U_{-\alpha,-\ell}\rangle

and
H_{\ell,-\ell}:=L_{\ell,-\ell}\cap T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
. Then

L_{\ell,-\ell}=(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell+})\cup(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.27
,
m(u)\in U_{-\alpha,-\ell}U_{\alpha,\ell}U_{-\alpha,-\ell}
and thus

B:=(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell+})\cup(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell})\subseteq L_{\ell,-\ell}.

By Lemma
5.31
,
m(u)\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,-\ell}
, which shows that
L_{\ell,-\ell}
is generated by
U_{\alpha,\ell}
and
m(u)
. Thus to see
L_{\ell,-\ell}\subseteq B
, it suffices to show that
B
is a group.
By Proposition
5.34
,
B\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}=B
and
B\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell+}=B
. By Lemma
5.25
,
B\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}=B
. It remains to show that
B\cdot m(u)=B
.

Since
m(u)\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,-\ell}
and by Lemma
5.30
,
m(u)\cdot T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)\cdot m(u)^{-1}=T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, we have

(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell})m(u)=U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\subseteq B

and

\displaystyle\left(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\right)m(u)=U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell}\cdot m(u)^{2}.

By Proposition
5.28
,
m(u)^{2}
acts trivially on
\mathbb{A}
, hence it lies in
\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap G_{\mathbb{F}}(O)=T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, so

\left(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\right)m(u)=U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell}.

If
u^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha,-\ell})_{\mathbb{F}}
satisfies
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=-\ell
, then by Lemma
5.29
,
m(u)^{-1}m(u^{\prime})\in T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, so
m(u^{\prime})\in m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}
since
m(u),m(u^{\prime})\in L_{\ell,-\ell}
. Thus

u^{\prime}\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u^{\prime})\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\subseteq U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}.

and

U_{-\alpha,-\ell}\subseteq U_{-\alpha,-\ell+}\cup U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}.

This shows

\displaystyle\left(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\right)m(u)

\displaystyle\subseteq\left(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell+}\right)\cup\left(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=B.

Similarly, one can show
B\cdot m(u)^{-1}\subseteq B
, which concludes the proof of
B=L_{\ell,-\ell}
.
∎

Proposition 5.36
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{A}
be a non-empty finite subset and

\ell=\min_{a.o\in\Omega}\{(-v)(\chi_{\alpha}(a))\},\quad\ell^{\prime}=\min_{a.o\in\Omega}\{(-v)(\chi_{-\alpha}(a))\}.

Let

L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=\langle u\in U_{\alpha,\ell}\cup U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\rangle

and
N_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:=L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})
. Then

L_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}=U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}\cdot N_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}.

Proof.

Since
\Omega\neq\emptyset
, we have
\ell+\ell^{\prime}\leq 0
. If
\ell+\ell^{\prime}<0
we can apply Proposition
5.34
and are done. Otherwise, we have

L_{\ell,-\ell}=(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{-\alpha,-\ell+})\cup(U_{\alpha,\ell}\cdot m(u)\cdot H_{\ell,-\ell}\cdot U_{\alpha,\ell})

by Proposition
5.35
. As
H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\subseteq T_{\mathbb{F}}(O)
, we can apply Lemma
5.25
to obtain
H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}U_{\alpha,\ell}=U_{\alpha,\ell}H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
and
H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}=U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}H_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
. Finally, Lemma
5.31
gives
m(u)U_{\alpha,\ell}m(u)^{-1}=U_{-\alpha,\ell-2\varphi_{\alpha}(u)}=U_{-\alpha,-\ell}=U_{-\alpha,\ell^{\prime}}
.
∎

We will prove a ’mixed Iwasawa’-decomposition for the rank one subgroup
(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
defined in
[
App25
, Section
]
. The compact group
K_{\mathbb{F}}
in the Iwasawa decomposition is replaced by a subgroup of
\operatorname{Stab}_{(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}}(o)
and an element
m
representing the non-trivial element of the spherical Weyl group
W_{\pm\alpha}=\{[\operatorname{Id}],[m]\}
of
(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proposition 5.37
.

Let
L_{o}=\langle U_{\alpha,o},U_{-\alpha,o}\rangle
. Then

\displaystyle L_{\pm\alpha}

\displaystyle=(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o}\ \amalg\ (U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Cen}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot m\cdot L_{o}.

Proof.

It is clear that the inclusion
\supseteq
holds. For the reverse, we use
[
App25
, Corollary
]
to write

(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\ \amalg\ (B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}m(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

where
m
is a representative of the non-trivial element in the spherical Weyl group
W_{\pm\alpha}
associated with
(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
as in
[
App25
, Section
]
. If
g\in(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
, then
g\in U_{\alpha}(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})
. We claim that

m(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}=m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o}.

Assuming the claim we obtain

\displaystyle(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}m(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\subseteq(B_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o}

\displaystyle\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o}

since
(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
and
(A_{\pm\alpha})
normalize
(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. To prove the claim, we consider an element in
m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u
for
u\in(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
. If
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)\leq 0
, then
m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})L_{o}
and we are done. If on the other hand
\varphi_{\alpha}(u)>0
, then there are
u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
with
u=u^{\prime}\cdot m(u)\cdot u^{\prime\prime}
and
\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime})=\varphi_{-\alpha}(u^{\prime\prime})=-\varphi_{\alpha}(u)<0
by Lemma
5.27
. In particular
u^{\prime\prime}\in L_{o}
and

\displaystyle m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u

\displaystyle=m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}u^{\prime}\cdot m(u)\cdot u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=m\tilde{u}^{\prime}m^{-1}\cdot m(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}m(u)\cdot u^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\subseteq(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\cdot(\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap(L_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}})\cdot L_{o},

where
\tilde{u}^{\prime}\in(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
is a
(M_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}(A_{\pm\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}
-conjugate of
u^{\prime}
.
∎

The result of Proposition
5.36
still holds, when
\Omega=\emptyset
.

Lemma 5.38
.

Let
\alpha\in\Sigma
,
L=\langle(U_{\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}},(U_{-\alpha})_{\mathbb{F}}\rangle
and

N=\operatorname{Nor}_{G_{\mathbb{F}}}(A_{\mathbb{F}})\cap L
. Then
